Now precomputing M, L, O, D: We need a forward-like kernel to compute O, M, L. **forward_kernel**: For each (b, h, q_tile): - Load Q tile. - Loop over kv_tiles: - Load K tile. - Compute S = Q @ K^T * scale (like above). - Track row max and sum. - After the loop, compute softmax denominators L and output O = P @ V. Let's write the forward kernel similarly: ``` __global__ void fwd_kernel(..., __nv_bfloat16* O, float* M, float* L) { // block: (q_tile, h, b) // load Q tile to smem. // row_max[1] = -inf; row_sum[1] = 0; // Loop kv tiles: // load K, V. // compute S row. // update row_max, row_sum online using softmax scaling. // compute O accumulator: O_new = O_old * exp(old_max - new_max) + sum(V * exp(S - new_max)) // After loop: // O_final = O_acc / row_sum // L = row_sum // M = row_max } ``` This is the standard FlashAttention forward online softmax algorithm. It's not too hard to implement. Wait, do I need O for anything other than D? The hidden reference might just compute gradients. But yes, D = dot(dO, O). We can write helper functions: ``` __device__ __forceinline__ void load_bfloat16x2(const __nv_bfloat16* src, __nv_bfloat16* dst, int count) { // count is multiple of 2 const __nv_bfloat162* s2 = reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat162*>(src); __nv_bfloat162* d2 = reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat162*>(dst); for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < count/2; i += blockDim.x) { d2[i] = s2[i]; } } ``` But we need to be careful about alignment. If the tensor strides are such that rows are contiguous (which they are since we use `.contiguous()` in PyTorch), then each row is aligned to at least 2 bytes, but `__nv_bfloat162` requires 4-byte alignment. For example: ``` float qk_dot() { float sum = 0; for (int d = 0; d < D; d += 2) { __nv_bfloat162 q2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)&Q_smem[row*D + d]; __nv_bfloat162 k2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)&K_smem[col*D + d]; sum += __bfloat162float(q2.x) * __bfloat162float(k2.x); sum += __bfloat162float(q2.y) * __bfloat162float(k2.y); } } ``` This is much faster. Numerical precision: Using float for accumulators is good. The reference uses cutlass::half_t and probably similar accumulation. 2. Softmax: We need to make sure `m` and `l` are correct. If each thread computes a different 16-col slice of S, they have different p values for those c's. But the O accumulation sums over all c's. This is cleaner: - Partition c dimension across the 4 threads. - Each thread computes partial reductions for the row-level outputs (O, dQ, dK, dV). - Use warp shuffle to sum across the 4 threads. - Only lane 0 (or c_group 0) writes to global memory. This avoids all duplicate compute and write conflicts. Let's define: ``` int tid = threadIdx.x; int row = tid >> 2; // 0..63 int c_group = tid & 3; // 0..3 int c_start = c_group << 4; // 0, 16, 32, 48 ``` **Forward kernel with c_group partitioning:** ``` float o_acc[128] = {0.0f}; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; for (int kv_tile=0; kv_tile < num_kv_tiles; ++kv_tile) { int kv_start = kv_tile * Bc; // load K, V to smem __syncthreads(); // compute S for c in [c_start, c_start+16) float s_reg[16]; for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) { int c = c_start + ci; // dot product over D float sum = 0.0f; const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = Q_smem + row * D; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K_smem + c * D; #pragma unroll for (int d=0; d<D; d+=2) { __nv_bfloat162 q2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + d); __nv_bfloat162 k2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + d); sum += __bfloat162float(q2.x) * __bfloat162float(k2.x); sum += __bfloat162float(q2.y) * __bfloat162float(k2.y); } s_reg[ci] = sum * scale; } // Find max across c_group (16 vals) for this thread, then warp reduce across 4 threads float local_m = -INFINITY; for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) local_m = fmaxf(local_m, s_reg[ci]); // reduce across c_group using shuffle #pragma unroll for (int offset = 1; offset < 4; offset <<= 1) { float other = __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, offset); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, other); } // broadcast local_m to all 4 threads in the row float m_new = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, (row << 2)); // get from lane row*4? Actually lane 0 of the group. // Wait, lane id = tid. For row r, lanes are r*4, r*4+1, r*4+2, r*4+3. // __shfl_sync from lane r*4: float m_new = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, row * 4); ``` Wait, `__shfl_sync` takes lane index. If each group of 4 lanes needs the max, we first compute within the group: ``` // group base lane int base_lane = (tid >> 2) << 2; // tid & ~3 // group reduction float m_new = local_m; m_new = fmaxf(m_new, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, m_new, 1)); m_new = fmaxf(m_new, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, m_new, 2)); // Now all 4 lanes have the same m_new (max over 64 columns). ``` Yes, `__shfl_xor_sync(mask, var, 1)` swaps with neighbor, `2` swaps with lane +/-2. After both, all 4 have the max. Great. Then we do the online softmax: ``` float alpha = expf(m - m_new); m = m_new; l *= alpha; for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) { float p = expf(s_reg[ci] - m); l += p; // only sum over 16 cols per thread, need warp reduction? } ``` Wait, `l` is the sum over all 64 columns. Each thread sums its 16 p's locally, then we need to reduce across the 4 threads. ``` float local_l = 0; for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) local_l += expf(s_reg[ci] - m); // reduce across group local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 1); local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 2); ``` Then all threads have `l_new = l * alpha + local_l`. Since all threads need the same `l`, we can just let each thread compute it (since inputs are identical after shuffle). Then update O_acc: ``` for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) { float p = expf(s_reg[ci] - m); // same p computed before int c = c_start + ci; for (int d=0; d<D; ++d) { o_acc[d] += p * __bfloat162float(V_smem[c*D + d]); } } // But wait, we need to apply alpha to previous o_acc! ``` Actually the online softmax update should be: ``` for (int d=0; d<D; ++d) o_acc[d] *= alpha; for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) { float p = expf(s_reg[ci] - m); int c = c_start + ci; for (int d=0; d<D; ++d) { o_acc[d] += p * V[c][d]; } } ``` After all kv_tiles: ``` // l and m are final. for (int d=0; d<D; ++d) { // reduce o_acc across 4 threads in the row float val = o_acc[d]; val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 1); val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 2); if (c_group == 0) { O_ptr[row*D + d] = __float2bfloat16(val / l); } } if (c_group == 0) { M_ptr[row] = m; L_ptr[row] = l; } ``` This is very clean and has no duplicate computation. For the backward kernels, similar partitioning. **dq kernel:** Mapping: row = tid / 4, c_group = tid % 4, c_start = c_group * 16. Global: q_tile row = q_start + row. Wait, does this work? The sum `dQ[row][d] = sum_{c=0..63} ds[row][c] * K[c][d]`. If each thread handles c_start to c_start+15, it computes a partial sum for its d_group of 32. Let's formalize: **Forward kernel code plan:** ``` int tid = threadIdx.x; int row = tid >> 2; int c_group = tid & 3; int c_start = c_group << 4; // *16 float o_acc[128]; #pragma unroll for (int i=0; i<128; ++i) o_acc[i] = 0.0f; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; for (int kv_tile=0; kv_tile < num_kv_tiles; ++kv_tile) { __syncthreads(); // load K and V tile by all threads for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / D; int d = idx % D; int g_idx = kv_start + r; K_smem[idx] = K_ptr[g_idx * D + d]; V_smem[idx] = V_ptr[g_idx * D + d]; } __syncthreads(); // compute s for c_start..c_start+15 float s_reg[16]; #pragma unroll for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) { int c = c_start + ci; float sum = 0.0f; const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = Q_smem + row * D; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K_smem + c * D; #pragma unroll for (int d=0; d<D; d+=2) { __nv_bfloat162 q2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + d); __nv_bfloat162 k2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + d); sum += __bfloat162float(q2.x) * __bfloat162float(k2.x); sum += __bfloat162float(q2.y) * __bfloat162float(k2.y); } s_reg[ci] = sum * scale; } // find local max float local_m = -INFINITY; #pragma unroll for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) local_m = fmaxf(local_m, s_reg[ci]); // reduce max across c_group local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 1)); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 2)); float m_new = local_m; float alpha = expf(m - m_new); m = m_new; // update o_acc #pragma unroll for (int i=0; i<128; ++i) o_acc[i] *= alpha; // compute p and sum l float local_l = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int ci=0; ci<16; ++ci) { float p = expf(s_reg[ci] - m); local_l += p; int c = c_start + ci; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V_smem + c * D; #pragma unroll for (int d=0; d<D; ++d) { o_acc[d] += p * __bfloat162float(v_ptr[d]); } } // reduce l local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 1); local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 2); l = l * alpha + local_l; } // normalize and write #pragma unroll for (int d=0; d<D; ++d) { float val = o_acc[d]; val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 1); val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 2); val /= l; if (c_group == 0) { O_ptr[row * D + d] = __float2bfloat16(val); } } if (c_group == 0) { M_ptr[row] = m; L_ptr[row] = l; } ``` Wait, `v_ptr[d]` access for each `d`: all threads in a warp access `V_smem + c*D + d` for different `c` and possibly same `d`. Actually warp 0: tid 0..31. row = tid/4 = 0..7. c_group = tid%4. So within warp, threads have different rows and different c_groups. Access to V_smem is scattered. Bank conflicts could occur. Let's define: ``` constexpr int D = 128; constexpr int SMEM_STRIDE = 130; // D + 2 constexpr int Br = 64; constexpr int Bc = 64; ``` Helper for dot product using bfloat162: ``` __device__ __forceinline__ float dot_bf16(const __nv_bfloat16* a, const __nv_bfloat16* b) { float sum = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < D; d += 2) { __nv_bfloat162 av = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(a + d); __nv_bfloat162 bv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(b + d); sum += __bfloat162float(av.x) * __bfloat162float(bv.x); sum += __bfloat162float(av.y) * __bfloat162float(bv.y); } return sum; } ``` Note: `a + d` pointer arithmetic: `d` counts elements. `a + d` points to element `d`. Casting to `__nv_bfloat162*` requires `d` even (it is). `a + d + 1` would be misaligned. Now the forward kernel: ``` template<int D, int SMEM_STRIDE> __global__ __launch_bounds__(256, 3) void fwd_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, float* __restrict__ M, float* __restrict__ L, int B, int H, int H_kv, int N, float scale) { int b = blockIdx.z; int h = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int q_start = q_tile * Br; int tid = threadIdx.x; int row = tid % 64; // 0..63 int c_group = tid / 64; // 0..3 int c_start = c_group * 16; int h_kv = h / (H / H_kv); const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = Q + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * D; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * D; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * D; __nv_bfloat16* o_ptr = O + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * D; float* m_ptr = M + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); float* l_ptr = L + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); extern __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* Q_smem = smem; __nv_bfloat16* K_smem = smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* V_smem = K_smem + Bc * SMEM_STRIDE; // Load Q tile for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 2); int c = (idx % (D / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + r * D + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = val; } float o_acc[D]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < D; ++i) o_acc[i] = 0.0f; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = N / Bc; // exact for (int kv_tile = 0; kv_tile < num_kv_tiles; ++kv_tile) { int kv_start = kv_tile * Bc; __syncthreads(); // Load K and V tiles for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 2); int c = (idx % (D / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 valk = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + (kv_start + r) * D + c); __nv_bfloat162 valv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(v_ptr + (kv_start + r) * D + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = valk; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = valv; } __syncthreads(); // Compute S for this row and c_group slice float s_reg[16]; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { int c = c_start + ci; s_reg[ci] = dot_bf16(Q_smem + row * SMEM_STRIDE, K_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE) * scale; } // Online softmax: find m_new across c_group float local_m = -INFINITY; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) local_m = fmaxf(local_m, s_reg[ci]); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 1)); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 2)); float m_new = local_m; float alpha = expf(m - m_new); m = m_new; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < D; ++i) o_acc[i] *= alpha; float local_l = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { float p = expf(s_reg[ci] - m); local_l += p; int c = c_start + ci; const __nv_bfloat16* v_row = V_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < D; ++d) { o_acc[d] += p * __bfloat162float(v_row[d]); } } local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 1); local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 2); l = l * alpha + local_l; } // Normalize and write #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < D; ++d) { float val = o_acc[d]; val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 1); val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 2); val /= l; if (c_group == 0) { o_ptr[row * D + d] = __float2bfloat16(val); } } if (c_group == 0) { m_ptr[row] = m; l_ptr[row] = l; } } ``` Wait, `dot_bf16` is defined with `#pragma unroll` inside. For a given q row `q`, thread computes: - `s = dot(Q[q], K[kv_row]) * scale` -> single float scalar. - `dp = dot(dO[q], V[kv_row])` -> single float. These dot products require the entire K and V row. Then dot product s: each thread can compute its partial dot product over its pair, then reduce via warp shuffle across the 64 threads of that row. ``` float local_dot = 0; #pragma unroll for (int d_pair = pair_start; d_pair < 64; d_pair += ?) ``` Wait, if we have 64 threads per row, each can handle one pair. We need to reduce across 2 warps. We can do warp shuffle within each warp, then combine the two warp sums via shared memory. Reduction of 64 partial sums: - Each thread computes `my_val = q2.x * k2.x + q2.y * k2.y`. - Warp 0 (threads 0..31): `__shfl_down_sync` reduce to lane 0. - Warp 1 (threads 32..63): reduce to lane 32. - Write warp sums to smem (2 floats per row). - Thread 0 reads and sums. This is a bit of extra logic but doable. Since there are 4 rows, we need 4 reductions. Alternatively, we could compute the dot product using a shared memory accumulation: - Initialize `float red_smem[4];` to 0. - Each thread `atomicAdd(&red_smem[row_offset], my_val);` - After all threads add, thread 0 reads `red_smem[row_offset]`. But we need one red_smem per row. `red_smem[4]`. That's tiny. And atomicAdd on smem is fast. This is much simpler! Let's use `__shared__ float red_s[4];` and `__shared__ float red_dp[4];`. For each q row: - Each thread computes `local_s = q2.x * k2.x + q2.y * k2.y;` (its partial contribution to dot product). - `atomicAdd(&red_s[row_offset], local_s);` - Similarly `local_dp = do2.x * v2.x + do2.y * v2.y;` - `atomicAdd(&red_dp[row_offset], local_dp);` - `__syncthreads();` - Thread 0 (or one thread per row) reads `red_s[row_offset]` and computes `s = red_s * scale`, etc. - Broadcast s and dp to all threads. Since all threads in a row need the same s and dp, we can have thread `row_offset*64` read it and store into a smem broadcast location, or use shuffle: `s = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, red_val, row_offset*64);` Wait, row_offset*64 might not be in the same warp for all threads in the row. Actually row_offset=0 threads are 0..63 (warps 0 and 1). `__shfl_sync` works across the whole block (any lane) in compute >= 8.0? Actually on Volta+, `__shfl_sync` works within a warp only. You cannot shuffle across warps. So to broadcast from lane 0 of warp 1 to warp 0, you can't use shuffle. Use smem for broadcast: ``` __shared__ float s_smem[4]; __shared__ float dp_smem[4]; if (threadIdx.x == row_offset) { // only one thread per row reads atomic result s_smem[row_offset] = red_s[row_offset] * scale; dp_smem[row_offset] = red_dp[row_offset]; } __syncthreads(); float s = s_smem[row_offset]; float dp = dp_smem[row_offset]; ``` Then all threads use `s` and `dp` to compute `p`, `ds`, and accumulate into their dk/dv. After accumulation, clear red_s and red_dp for next q row. ``` if (threadIdx.x < 4) { red_s[threadIdx.x] = 0; red_dp[threadIdx.x] = 0; } __syncthreads(); ``` This is clean and register-efficient. Let's verify total smem: - Q_smem: 16640 - dO_smem: 16640 - K_smem: 4 * SMEM_STRIDE * 2 = 4 * 130 * 2 = 1040 - V_smem: 1040 - red_s: 4 * 4 = 16 - red_dp: 16 - s_smem: 16 - dp_smem: 16 Total: ~35KB. 6 blocks per SM = 210KB. 256 threads per block. No. So 6 blocks per SM = 75% occupancy. This is very good for latency hiding. Total blocks: N/4 * H_kv * B = 256 * 8 * 2 = 4096 blocks. 4096 blocks / (128 SMs * 6 blocks/SM) = 5.3 waves. Fine. So the dkdv kernel with 4 kv_rows per block and atomic reduction in smem is a great design for H100. Let's finalize the dkdv kernel. **dkdv kernel:** ``` template<int D, int SMEM_STRIDE> __global__ void dkdv_kernel(...) { int b = blockIdx.z; int h_kv = blockIdx.y; int kv_tile = blockIdx.x; int kv_start = kv_tile * 4; // 4 rows per block int tid = threadIdx.x; int row_offset = tid / 64; // 0..3 (which of the 4 rows) int pair_idx = tid % 64; // 0..63 (which column pair) int d = pair_idx * 2; int abs_kv_row = kv_start + row_offset; int h_start = h_kv * (H / H_kv); int G = H / H_kv; const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = Q + (b * H) * N * D; const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dO + (b * H) * N * D; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * D; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * D; __nv_bfloat16* dk_ptr = dK + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * D; __nv_bfloat16* dv_ptr = dV + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * D; // Load K and V rows into registers (pairs) __nv_bfloat162 k2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + d); __nv_bfloat162 v2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(v_ptr + d); extern __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* Q_smem = smem; __nv_bfloat16* dO_smem = smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* K_smem = dO_smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; // for dot product reduction (optional) __nv_bfloat16* V_smem = K_smem + 4 * SMEM_STRIDE; // Actually we don't need K_smem and V_smem in shared mem since we have k2,v2 in reg. // But for dot product reduction, we need to accumulate partial products. // We can just accumulate partial products locally and atomicAdd to float smem. // Let's allocate float scratch at the end: } ``` Actually, we don't need K_smem and V_smem. We only need Q and dO tiles. And float scratch. Smem layout: - Q_smem: Br * SMEM_STRIDE - dO_smem: Br * SMEM_STRIDE - red_s: 4 floats - red_dp: 4 floats - s_smem: 4 floats - dp_smem: 4 floats Total: 2 * 64 * 130 * 2 + 4*4*4 = 33280 + 64 = 33344 bytes (~32.6KB). Wait, 6 blocks = 195KB. Good. Wait, `Q_smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE` where `Br=64`. 64 * 130 * 2 = 16640. So total ~33KB. Good. We can allocate red_s etc as float arrays: ``` float* red_s = (float*)(smem + 2 * Br * SMEM_STRIDE); float* red_dp = red_s + 4; float* s_smem = red_dp + 4; float* dp_smem = s_smem + 4; ``` But careful with alignment. `smem` is `__nv_bfloat16*`. `2 * Br * SMEM_STRIDE` = 33280 bytes. This is divisible by 4. Casting to `float*` is fine. Now kernel steps: ``` float dk_x = 0.0f, dk_y = 0.0f; float dv_x = 0.0f, dv_y = 0.0f; for (int g = 0; g < G; ++g) { int h = h_start + g; const __nv_bfloat16* q_head_ptr = q_base + h * N * D; const __nv_bfloat16* do_head_ptr = do_base + h * N * D; const float* m_ptr = M + ((b * H + h) * N); const float* l_ptr = L + ((b * H + h) * N); const float* d_vec_ptr = D_vec + ((b * H + h) * N); for (int q_tile = 0; q_tile < (N / Br); ++q_tile) { int q_start = q_tile * Br; __syncthreads(); // Load Q and dO tiles for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 2); int c = (idx % (D / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 q2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_head_ptr + (q_start + r) * D + c); __nv_bfloat162 do2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(do_head_ptr + (q_start + r) * D + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = q2; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dO_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = do2; } __syncthreads(); // Initialize red to 0 if (tid < 4) { red_s[tid] = 0.0f; red_dp[tid] = 0.0f; } __syncthreads(); for (int q_row = 0; q_row < Br; ++q_row) { // compute partial dot products __nv_bfloat162 q2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + q_row * SMEM_STRIDE + d); __nv_bfloat162 do2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dO_smem + q_row * SMEM_STRIDE + d); float local_s = __bfloat162float(q2.x) * __bfloat162float(k2.x) + __bfloat162float(q2.y) * __bfloat162float(k2.y); float local_dp = __bfloat162float(do2.x) * __bfloat162float(v2.x) + __bfloat162float(do2.y) * __bfloat162float(v2.y); atomicAdd(&red_s[row_offset], local_s); atomicAdd(&red_dp[row_offset], local_dp); __syncthreads(); if (tid == row_offset) { s_smem[row_offset] = red_s[row_offset] * scale; dp_smem[row_offset] = red_dp[row_offset]; // reset for next q_row red_s[row_offset] = 0.0f; red_dp[row_offset] = 0.0f; } __syncthreads(); float s_val = s_smem[row_offset]; float dp_val = dp_smem[row_offset]; float m_val = m_ptr[q_start + q_row]; float l_val = l_ptr[q_start + q_row]; float d_val = d_vec_ptr[q_start + q_row]; float p_val = expf(s_val - m_val) / l_val; float ds_val = p_val * (dp_val - d_val) * scale; dk_x += ds_val * __bfloat162float(q2.x); dk_y += ds_val * __bfloat162float(q2.y); dv_x += p_val * __bfloat162float(do2.x); dv_y += p_val * __bfloat162float(do2.y); } // end q_row } // end q_tile } // end g // write results __nv_bfloat162 dk2, dv2; dk2.x = __float2bfloat16(dk_x); dk2.y = __float2bfloat16(dk_y); dv2.x = __float2bfloat16(dv_x); dv2.y = __float2bfloat16(dv_y); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dk_ptr + d) = dk2; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dv_ptr + d) = dv2; ``` Wait, there's a correctness issue: in the q_row loop, we do `atomicAdd` then `__syncthreads`. After the `__syncthreads`, the values in `red_s` are final. Then one thread per row reads and resets. Then another `__syncthreads`. Then all threads read `s_smem`. This is correct. But note: `q2` and `do2` are loaded from smem before the sync. They are per thread. Different threads have different `d`. The `atomicAdd` accumulates local dot products. Then after sync, `s_val` broadcast. Then dk accumulations use the same `q2` and `do2`. This is correct. Potential issue: `atomicAdd` on float shared memory. Is it available? Yes, `atomicAdd(float*, float)` works in shared memory on sm_60+. H100 is sm_90. Good. But the repeated `__syncthreads()` inside the inner `q_row` loop (64 iterations) might add overhead. 64 * 2 = 128 syncs per q_tile. 16 q_tiles * 4 q_heads = 64 * 128 = 8192 syncs per block. Per block, many syncs. Shared memory atomics are fast. Can we reduce syncs? We could have a reduction without atomics using warp shuffles, but the 64 threads are in 2 warps. We can do: - Warp 0 (lane 0..31): `__shfl_down_sync` to lane 0. - Warp 1 (lane 32..63): `__shfl_down_sync` to lane 32. - Then we need cross-warp exchange. We can write lane 0 sum to smem[0], lane 32 sum to smem[1]. Then one thread reads both and writes to s_smem. This avoids atomicAdd but still requires syncs for cross-warp. Actually, we can have each warp reduce internally without sync, then write to smem, then `__syncthreads()`, then one thread sums and writes to `s_smem`. This is still 1 sync per q_row. The atomic approach also requires 2 syncs per q_row. So warp reduce + 1 sync is better. Let's implement warp reduction: ``` float local_s = ...; // reduce within warp #pragma unroll for (int offset = 32; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { local_s += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_s, offset); } if ((tid & 31) == 0) { // lane 0 of each warp writes to smem red_s[warp_id] = local_s; red_dp[warp_id] = local_dp; } __syncthreads(); if (tid == 0) { float sum_s = red_s[0] + red_s[1]; float sum_dp = red_dp[0] + red_dp[1]; s_smem[row_offset] = sum_s * scale; dp_smem[row_offset] = sum_dp; } __syncthreads(); ``` Here `warp_id` = `tid / 32`. Since 64 threads per row, there are 2 warps per row. `red_s` needs to be size `4 * 2` = 8? Actually each row has 2 warps. 4 rows -> 8 warps total. But we only need to store 2 partial sums per row. So `red_s[2]` per row? Simpler: `red_s[8]` for 8 warps. Then after sync, a designated thread per row (e.g., tid = row_offset * 64) reads the two warp sums and adds them. Wait, mapping: row_offset = tid / 64. warp_id_in_block = tid / 32. Warp 0: row 0, lanes 0..31. Warp 1: row 0, lanes 32..63. Warp 2: row 1, lanes 64..95. Warp 3: row 1, lanes 96..127. Warp 4: row 2, lanes 128..159. Warp 5: row 2, lanes 160..191. Warp 6: row 3, lanes 192..223. Warp 7: row 3, lanes 224..255. So we can have `red_s[8]` and `red_dp[8]`. After warp reduction, each warp 0/1 writes to `red_s[warp_id]` and `red_dp[warp_id]`. Then `__syncthreads()`. Then thread `row_offset * 64` reads `red_s[row_offset*2]` and `red_s[row_offset*2+1]`, sums them, writes to `s_smem[row_offset]`. Then `__syncthreads()`. Only 1 sync per q_row. And no atomics! This is better. Let's refine: ``` __shared__ float red_s[8]; __shared__ float red_dp[8]; __shared__ float s_smem[4]; __shared__ float dp_smem[4]; for each q_row: // compute local_s, local_dp int warp_id = tid >> 5; // 0..7 // warp reduce #pragma unroll for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { local_s += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_s, offset); local_dp += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_dp, offset); } if ((tid & 31) == 0) { red_s[warp_id] = local_s; red_dp[warp_id] = local_dp; } __syncthreads(); if (tid == row_offset * 64) { float sum_s = red_s[row_offset * 2] + red_s[row_offset * 2 + 1]; float sum_dp = red_dp[row_offset * 2] + red_dp[row_offset * 2 + 1]; s_smem[row_offset] = sum_s * scale; dp_smem[row_offset] = sum_dp; } __syncthreads(); float s_val = s_smem[row_offset]; float dp_val = dp_smem[row_offset]; // ... accumulate dk/dv ``` This works and is clean. We can even skip `red_s` array and have warp 0 write to `s_smem[row_offset * 2]` and warp 1 write to `s_smem[row_offset * 2 + 1]` if `s_smem` is size 8. Then after sync, one thread sums two adjacent entries and writes back. But we need two arrays for s and dp. So using `red_s` and `red_dp` is fine. So close. If we don't use `red_dp` array but reuse `dp_smem`? We can write warp sums directly into `dp_smem[8]` and then one thread reads two entries and writes back to `dp_smem[row_offset]`. We don't need both `red_dp` and `dp_smem`. Similarly for s. Let's try: - `warp_sum_s[8];` - `warp_sum_dp[8];` - After warp reduction, write to warp_sum_s[warp_id]. - `__syncthreads();` - Thread `row_offset*64` reads warp_sum_s[warp_offset*2], warp_sum_s[warp_offset*2+1], sums, writes to `s_val_smem[row_offset]`. - Then `__syncthreads();` - All threads read `s_val_smem[row_offset]`. We need `s_val_smem[4]` and `dp_val_smem[4]`. Total float smem: 8 + 8 + 4 + 4 = 24 floats = 96 bytes. Same as before. What if we use `s_val_smem[8]` and `dp_val_smem[8]`. Warp 0 writes to `[row_offset*2]`, warp 1 writes to `[row_offset*2+1]`. Then thread `row_offset*64` reads the two and overwrites `[row_offset]` with the sum. So we need 8 floats for each during the intermediate step, but we can reuse the same array if we are careful? Not easily with a sync in between. Actually, we can do: ``` __shared__ float buf_s[8]; __shared__ float buf_dp[8]; // warp reduce if (lane_id == 0) { buf_s[warp_id] = local_s; buf_dp[warp_id] = local_dp; } __syncthreads(); if (tid == row_offset * 64) { buf_s[row_offset] = (buf_s[row_offset*2] + buf_s[row_offset*2+1]) * scale; buf_dp[row_offset] = buf_dp[row_offset*2] + buf_dp[row_offset*2+1]; } __syncthreads(); float s_val = buf_s[row_offset]; float dp_val = buf_dp[row_offset]; ``` We reuse the first 4 entries of `buf_s` and `buf_dp` to store the final results after reading adjacent entries. Since all warps are done writing before the sync, and then only one thread writes, it's safe. Total: 8*4 + 8*4 = 64 bytes. Total smem: 32768 + 64 = 32832 bytes (if SMEM_STRIDE=128). *7 = 229824 > 228KB. Still over by 1824 bytes. Wait, 228KB = 228 * 1024 = 233472 bytes. 229824 < 233472! Wait, 228*1024 = 233472. 229824 is indeed less than 233472. I miscalculated earlier. 228 KB = 233,472 bytes. 7 * 32832 = 229,824 bytes. This is LESS than 233,472. So 7 blocks fit! Wait, but I used SMEM_STRIDE=128 (no padding) to get 16384 per tile. Does that cause bank conflicts? Let's check. With SMEM_STRIDE=128 (no padding): Q_smem row start difference = 128*2=256 bytes. Bank diff = 256/4=64. 64%32=0. Same bank for same d across adjacent rows. Massive bank conflict in dot product when consecutive rows access same column. But in dkdv kernel, the dot product is per row: for a fixed q_row, threads across warps access the same row? No, in dkdv, at a given q_row, all threads in the block access the same q_row but different columns. With SMEM_STRIDE=128, consecutive columns d=0,1 bank0, d=2,3 bank1, etc. Threads with d=0,1 and d=64,65 both map to bank0. Since we have pair_idx 0..63, pair_idx=0 (d=0,1) and pair_idx=32 (d=64,65) are in different warps. In warp 0 (threads 0..31): pair_idx 0..31 cover d=0..63. Banks 0..31 (each bank accessed once). Warp 1 (threads 32..63): pair_idx 32..63 cover d=64..127. Banks 0..31 (each accessed once). So two warps access the same banks but in different warps. Since warps are scheduled independently, this is not a bank conflict within a single warp. Shared memory can sustain multiple warps accessing simultaneously if they are to different addresses? Actually, a bank can only serve one request per cycle. If warp 0 and warp 1 are issued in the same cycle and both access bank 0, there is a conflict (serialized). But warp schedulers usually issue one warp at a time per scheduler. H100 has 4 warp schedulers. They might issue warps concurrently. If they issue two warps that both hit bank 0, conflict. But it's only 2-way conflict. Acceptable. Similarly, dot product for q2 loads: within a warp, no conflict. Across warps, 2-way. Acceptable. So SMEM_STRIDE=128 might be okay! And it saves memory. But wait, in the forward kernel, consecutive rows are accessed by consecutive threads in a warp when loading Q? No, when loading Q into smem from global, it's fine because each thread writes a unique address. But when reading for dot product in forward: same row is accessed by threads in the same warp (tid 0..31 have row=0, c_group varies). Q_smem row access: all threads in warp 0 read row 0. Since c_group varies, they read different columns. pair_idx? No, in forward we don't use pair_idx; each thread handles all D? Wait, forward uses row and c_group. For dot product Q[row] dot K[c], threads in warp access same row but different K rows? No, they access same K row (since c is same for all threads in warp? Actually c_start differs by group: 0,16,32,48. So 4 different K rows. Q is same row broadcast. Q broadcast is fine. K rows: 4 different rows. Banks: row difference is c*256. Bank diff=0. So threads reading K rows 0,16,32,48 for same d map to same bank. Conflict! With SMEM_STRIDE=128, K row 0 and row 16 start at offsets 0 and 4096. 4096/4=1024. 1024%32=0. So same bank. 4-way conflict. Bad. With SMEM_STRIDE=130, offset diff = 16*260 = 4160. 4160/4=1040. 1040%32=16. Different bank. Good. So padding is essential for forward and dq kernels. For dkdv, we access a fixed q_row in Q_smem, so no cross-row conflict. Wait, in dkdv, at a given time, all threads access the same q_row but different columns. Adjacent rows are not accessed simultaneously. So SMEM_STRIDE=128 is okay for dkdv's Q_smem and dO_smem. But we can simply use SMEM_STRIDE=130 for all kernels to keep it uniform and avoid any conflict. The extra 256 bytes per tile is minor. Let's just go with the plan. **Summary of kernels:** 1. `fwd_kernel`: grid(num_q_tiles, H, B), block(256), smem(3 * 16640). Computes O, M, L. 2. `compute_d_kernel`: grid(ceil(total_rows / 256)), block(256). Computes D. 3. `dq_kernel`: grid(num_q_tiles, H, B), block(256), smem(4 * 16640). Computes dQ. 4. `dkdv_kernel`: grid(num_kv_tiles_with_4rows, H_kv, B), block(256), smem(2 * 16640 + 64). Computes dK,dV. Let me write the C++ code carefully. I will define `HEAD_DIM = 128`, `Br = 64`, `Bc = 64` (for forward/dq), `KV_ROWS_PER_BLOCK = 4` (for dkdv). `SMEM_STRIDE = HEAD_DIM + 2 = 130`. Wait, for dkdv, Q and dO tiles are loaded as full tiles of Br=64 rows. The block loops over q_tiles of 64 q rows each. This matches. I need to be very careful with pointer arithmetic and types. Also, I need to ensure `__nv_bfloat162` is available. In CUDA 11.0+, it is. On Hopper, definitely. One more thing: `__float2bfloat16` and `__bfloat162float` are available. **Code generation:** Because the code is extensive, I will structure it clearly. Also, the cpp_extension `load_inline` can take `extra_cuda_cflags=["-O3", "--use_fast_math", "-std=c++17", "-gencode=arch=compute_90,code=sm_90"]`. This ensures Hopper architecture. Let me draft the C++ source. ``` #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math.h> #define HEAD_DIM 128 #define SMEM_STRIDE 130 #define Br 64 #define Bc 64 #define KV_ROWS_PER_BLOCK 4 __device__ __forceinline__ float dot_bf16(const __nv_bfloat16* a, const __nv_bfloat16* b) { float sum = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; d += 2) { __nv_bfloat162 av = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(a + d); __nv_bfloat162 bv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(b + d); sum += __bfloat162float(av.x) * __bfloat162float(bv.x); sum += __bfloat162float(av.y) * __bfloat162float(bv.y); } return sum; } __global__ void fwd_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, float* __restrict__ M, float* __restrict__ L, int B, int H, int H_kv, int N, float scale) { int b = blockIdx.z; int h = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int q_start = q_tile * Br; int tid = threadIdx.x; int row = tid & 63; // tid % 64 int c_group = tid >> 6; // tid / 64 int c_start = c_group << 4; // *16 int h_kv = h / (H / H_kv); const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = Q + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * HEAD_DIM; __nv_bfloat16* o_ptr = O + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; float* m_ptr = M + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); float* l_ptr = L + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); extern __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* Q_smem = smem; __nv_bfloat16* K_smem = smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* V_smem = K_smem + Bc * SMEM_STRIDE; // Load Q tile for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + r * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = val; } float o_acc[HEAD_DIM]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < HEAD_DIM; ++i) o_acc[i] = 0.0f; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = N / Bc; for (int kv_tile = 0; kv_tile < num_kv_tiles; ++kv_tile) { int kv_start = kv_tile * Bc; __syncthreads(); for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 valk = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + (kv_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); __nv_bfloat162 valv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(v_ptr + (kv_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = valk; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = valv; } __syncthreads(); float s_reg[16]; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { int c = c_start + ci; s_reg[ci] = dot_bf16(Q_smem + row * SMEM_STRIDE, K_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE) * scale; } float local_m = -INFINITY; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) local_m = fmaxf(local_m, s_reg[ci]); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 1)); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 2)); float m_new = local_m; float alpha = expf(m - m_new); m = m_new; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < HEAD_DIM; ++i) o_acc[i] *= alpha; float local_l = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { float p = expf(s_reg[ci] - m); local_l += p; int c = c_start + ci; const __nv_bfloat16* v_row = V_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; ++d) { o_acc[d] += p * __bfloat162float(v_row[d]); } } local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 1); local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 2); l = l * alpha + local_l; } #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; ++d) { float val = o_acc[d]; val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 1); val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 2); val /= l; if (c_group == 0) { o_ptr[row * HEAD_DIM + d] = __float2bfloat16(val); } } if (c_group == 0) { m_ptr[row] = m; l_ptr[row] = l; } } ``` Wait, I need to check: after the forward kernel, `L` stores the sum of exp. In FlashAttention, `L` is sometimes logsumexp. But for our backward formulas, we used `p = exp(s - m) / l`, where `l` is the sum. So storing `l` is correct. Now D kernel: ``` __global__ void compute_d_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dO, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, float* __restrict__ D_vec, int total_rows) { int idx = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; if (idx >= total_rows) return; const __nv_bfloat16* do_ptr = dO + idx * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* o_ptr = O + idx * HEAD_DIM; float sum = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; d += 2) { __nv_bfloat162 do2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(do_ptr + d); __nv_bfloat162 o2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(o_ptr + d); sum += __bfloat162float(do2.x) * __bfloat162float(o2.x); sum += __bfloat162float(do2.y) * __bfloat162float(o2.y); } D_vec[idx] = sum; } ``` Now dq kernel: ``` __global__ void dq_kernel(...) { // block: (q_tile, h, b) int b = blockIdx.z; int h = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int q_start = q_tile * Br; int tid = threadIdx.x; int row = tid & 63; // 0..63 int c_group = tid >> 6; // 0..3 int c_start = c_group << 4; int h_kv = h / (H / H_kv); const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = Q + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* do_ptr = dO + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; __nv_bfloat16* dq_ptr = dQ + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; const float* m_ptr = M + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); const float* l_ptr = L + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); const float* d_vec_ptr = D_vec + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); extern __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* Q_smem = smem; __nv_bfloat16* dO_smem = smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* K_smem = dO_smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* V_smem = K_smem + Bc * SMEM_STRIDE; // Load Q, dO for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 qv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + r * HEAD_DIM + c); __nv_bfloat162 dov = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(do_ptr + r * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = qv; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dO_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = dov; } float m = m_ptr[row]; float l = l_ptr[row]; float d_val = d_vec_ptr[row]; float dq_acc[HEAD_DIM]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < HEAD_DIM; ++i) dq_acc[i] = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = N / Bc; for (int kv_tile = 0; kv_tile < num_kv_tiles; ++kv_tile) { int kv_start = kv_tile * Bc; __syncthreads(); for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 kv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + (kv_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); __nv_bfloat162 vv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(v_ptr + (kv_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = kv; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = vv; } __syncthreads(); #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { int c = c_start + ci; float s = dot_bf16(Q_smem + row * SMEM_STRIDE, K_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE) * scale; float p = expf(s - m) / l; float dp = dot_bf16(dO_smem + row * SMEM_STRIDE, V_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE); float ds = p * (dp - d_val) * scale; const __nv_bfloat16* k_row = K_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; ++d) { dq_acc[d] += ds * __bfloat162float(k_row[d]); } } } #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; ++d) { float val = dq_acc[d]; val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 1); val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 2); if (c_group == 0) { dq_ptr[row * HEAD_DIM + d] = __float2bfloat16(val); } } } ``` This looks correct. Now dkdv kernel. ``` __global__ void dkdv_kernel(...) { int b = blockIdx.z; int h_kv = blockIdx.y; int kv_base = blockIdx.x * KV_ROWS_PER_BLOCK; int tid = threadIdx.x; int row_offset = tid >> 6; // tid / 64, 0..3 int pair_idx = tid & 63; // 0..63 int d = pair_idx * 2; int abs_kv_row = kv_base + row_offset; int G = H / H_kv; int h_start = h_kv * G; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * HEAD_DIM; __nv_bfloat16* dk_ptr = dK + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * HEAD_DIM; __nv_bfloat16* dv_ptr = dV + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = Q + (b * H) * N * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dO + (b * H) * N * HEAD_DIM; // load K,V pair into registers __nv_bfloat162 k2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + d); __nv_bfloat162 v2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(v_ptr + d); extern __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* Q_smem = smem; __nv_bfloat16* dO_smem = Q_smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; float* buf_s = (float*)(smem + 2 * Br * SMEM_STRIDE); float* buf_dp = buf_s + 8; // using first 8 entries as scratch, then reuse lower 4 to store final sums. float dk_x = 0.0f, dk_y = 0.0f; float dv_x = 0.0f, dv_y = 0.0f; int num_q_tiles = N / Br; for (int g = 0; g < G; ++g) { int h = h_start + g; const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = q_base + h * N * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* do_ptr = do_base + h * N * HEAD_DIM; const float* m_ptr = M + ((b * H + h) * N); const float* l_ptr = L + ((b * H + h) * N); const float* d_vec_ptr = D_vec + ((b * H + h) * N); for (int q_tile = 0; q_tile < num_q_tiles; ++q_tile) { int q_start = q_tile * Br; __syncthreads(); for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 qv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + (q_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); __nv_bfloat162 dov = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(do_ptr + (q_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = qv; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dO_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = dov; } __syncthreads(); for (int q_row = 0; q_row < Br; ++q_row) { __nv_bfloat162 q2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + q_row * SMEM_STRIDE + d); __nv_bfloat162 do2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dO_smem + q_row * SMEM_STRIDE + d); float local_s = __bfloat162float(q2.x) * __bfloat162float(k2.x) + __bfloat162float(q2.y) * __bfloat162float(k2.y); float local_dp = __bfloat162float(do2.x) * __bfloat162float(v2.x) + __bfloat162float(do2.y) * __bfloat162float(v2.y); int lane = tid & 31; int warp_id = tid >> 5; #pragma unroll for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) { local_s += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_s, offset); local_dp += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_dp, offset); } if (lane == 0) { buf_s[warp_id] = local_s; buf_dp[warp_id] = local_dp; } __syncthreads(); if (tid == (row_offset << 6)) { // row_offset * 64 float sum_s = buf_s[row_offset << 1] + buf_s[(row_offset << 1) + 1]; float sum_dp = buf_dp[row_offset << 1] + buf_dp[(row_offset << 1) + 1]; buf_s[row_offset] = sum_s * scale; buf_dp[row_offset] = sum_dp; } __syncthreads(); float s_val = buf_s[row_offset]; float dp_val = buf_dp[row_offset]; float m_val = m_ptr[q_start + q_row]; float l_val = l_ptr[q_start + q_row]; float d_val = d_vec_ptr[q_start + q_row]; float p_val = expf(s_val - m_val) / l_val; float ds_val = p_val * (dp_val - d_val) * scale; dk_x += ds_val * __bfloat162float(q2.x); dk_y += ds_val * __bfloat162float(q2.y); dv_x += p_val * __bfloat162float(do2.x); dv_y += p_val * __bfloat162float(do2.y); } // q_row } // q_tile } // g __nv_bfloat162 dk2, dv2; dk2.x = __float2bfloat16(dk_x); dk2.y = __float2bfloat16(dk_y); dv2.x = __float2bfloat16(dv_x); dv2.y = __float2bfloat16(dv_y); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dk_ptr + d) = dk2; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dv_ptr + d) = dv2; } ``` Wait, there is a subtle bug: `buf_s` is shared memory of size 8 floats. We write warp sums to `buf_s[warp_id]`. Then thread `row_offset*64` sums `buf_s[row_offset*2]` and `buf_s[row_offset*2+1]`, and writes to `buf_s[row_offset]`. But `row_offset` can be 0..3. `buf_s[0]` originally held warp 0 sum. If row_offset=0, thread 0 reads buf_s[0] and buf_s[1] and writes back to buf_s[0]. That's fine because it's after the sync and no other thread will read buf_s[1] again. Similarly for row_offset=1, thread 64 writes to buf_s[1]. buf_s[1] previously held warp 2 (since warp_id for row 1 start is 2: threads 64..95 -> warp 2, threads 96..127 -> warp 3). assistant```python import torch import torch.nn as nn from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline CUDA_SOURCE = r""" #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <cmath> #define HEAD_DIM 128 #define SMEM_STRIDE 130 // HEAD_DIM + 2 to avoid shared-memory bank conflicts #define Br 64 #define Bc 64 #define KV_ROWS_PER_BLOCK 4 __device__ __forceinline__ float dot_bf16(const __nv_bfloat16* a, const __nv_bfloat16* b) { float sum = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; d += 2) { __nv_bfloat162 av = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(a + d); __nv_bfloat162 bv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(b + d); sum += __bfloat162float(av.x) * __bfloat162float(bv.x); sum += __bfloat162float(av.y) * __bfloat162float(bv.y); } return sum; } // ------------------------------------------------------------------ // Forward kernel: compute O, M (row max), L (row sum of exp) // ------------------------------------------------------------------ __global__ void fwd_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, float* __restrict__ M, float* __restrict__ L, int B, int H, int H_kv, int N, float scale) { int b = blockIdx.z; int h = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int q_start = q_tile * Br; int tid = threadIdx.x; int row = tid & 63; // tid % 64 int c_group = tid >> 6; // tid / 64 (0..3) int c_start = c_group << 4; // *16 int h_kv = h / (H / H_kv); const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = Q + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N) * HEAD_DIM; __nv_bfloat16* o_ptr = O + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; float* m_ptr = M + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); float* l_ptr = L + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start); extern __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* Q_smem = smem; __nv_bfloat16* K_smem = Q_smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* V_smem = K_smem + Bc * SMEM_STRIDE; // Load Q tile for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 val = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + r * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = val; } float o_acc[HEAD_DIM]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < HEAD_DIM; ++i) o_acc[i] = 0.0f; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = N / Bc; for (int kv_tile = 0; kv_tile < num_kv_tiles; ++kv_tile) { int kv_start = kv_tile * Bc; __syncthreads(); for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 valk = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(k_ptr + (kv_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); __nv_bfloat162 valv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(v_ptr + (kv_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = valk; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = valv; } __syncthreads(); float s_reg[16]; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { int c = c_start + ci; s_reg[ci] = dot_bf16(Q_smem + row * SMEM_STRIDE, K_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE) * scale; } float local_m = -INFINITY; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) local_m = fmaxf(local_m, s_reg[ci]); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 1)); local_m = fmaxf(local_m, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_m, 2)); float m_new = local_m; float alpha = expf(m - m_new); m = m_new; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < HEAD_DIM; ++i) o_acc[i] *= alpha; float local_l = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { float p = expf(s_reg[ci] - m); local_l += p; int c = c_start + ci; const __nv_bfloat16* v_row = V_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; ++d) { o_acc[d] += p * __bfloat162float(v_row[d]); } } local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 1); local_l += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_l, 2); l = l * alpha + local_l; } #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; ++d) { float val = o_acc[d]; val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 1); val += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 2); val /= l; if (c_group == 0) { o_ptr[row * HEAD_DIM + d] = __float2bfloat16(val); } } if (c_group == 0) { m_ptr[row] = m; l_ptr[row] = l; } } // ------------------------------------------------------------------ // D kernel: D_i = sum_d dout_i * o_i // ------------------------------------------------------------------ __global__ void compute_d_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dO, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ O, float* __restrict__ D_vec, int total_rows) { int idx = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; if (idx >= total_rows) return; const __nv_bfloat16* do_ptr = dO + idx * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* o_ptr = O + idx * HEAD_DIM; float sum = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; d += 2) { __nv_bfloat162 do2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(do_ptr + d); __nv_bfloat162 o2 = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(o_ptr + d); sum += __bfloat162float(do2.x) * __bfloat162float(o2.x); sum += __bfloat162float(do2.y) * __bfloat162float(o2.y); } D_vec[idx] = sum; } // ------------------------------------------------------------------ // dQ kernel // ------------------------------------------------------------------ __global__ void dq_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dO, const float* __restrict__ M, const float* __restrict__ L, const float* __restrict__ D_vec, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dQ, int B, int H, int H_kv, int N, float scale) { int b = blockIdx.z; int h = blockIdx.y; int q_tile = blockIdx.x; int q_start = q_tile * Br; int tid = threadIdx.x; int row = tid & 63; int c_group = tid >> 6; int c_start = c_group << 4; int h_kv = h / (H / H_kv); const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = Q + ((b * H + h) * N + q_start) * HEAD_DIM; extern __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem[]; __nv_bfloat16* Q_smem = smem; __nv_bfloat16* dO_smem = Q_smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* K_smem = dO_smem + Br * SMEM_STRIDE; __nv_bfloat16* V_smem = K_smem + Bc * SMEM_STRIDE; // Load Q, dO for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * HEAD_DIM / 2; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (HEAD_DIM / 2); int c = (idx % (HEAD_DIM / 2)) * 2; __nv_bfloat162 qv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(q_ptr + r * HEAD_DIM + c); __nv_bfloat162 dov = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(do_ptr + r * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(Q_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = qv; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(dO_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = dov; } float m = m_ptr[row]; float l = l_ptr[row]; float d_val = d_vec_ptr[row]; float dq_acc[HEAD_DIM]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < HEAD_DIM; ++i) dq_acc[i] = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = N / Bc; for (int kv_tile = 0; kv_tile < num_kv_tiles; __nv_bfloat162 vv = *(__nv_bfloat162*)(v_ptr + (kv_start + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); *(__nv_bfloat162*)(K_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = kv; *(__nv_bfloat162*)(V_smem + r * SMEM_STRIDE + c) = vv; } __syncthreads(); #pragma unroll for (int ci = 0; ci < 16; ++ci) { int c = c_start + ci; float s = dot_bf16(Q_smem + row * SMEM_STRIDE, K_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE) * scale; float p = expf(s - m) / l; float dp = dot_bf16(dO_smem + row * SMEM_STRIDE, V_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE); float ds = p * (dp - d_val) * scale; const __nv_bfloat16* k_row = K_smem + c * SMEM_STRIDE; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM; ++d) { dq_acc[d] += ds * __bfloat162float(k_row[d]); } } } #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; } } } // ------------------------------------------------------------------ // dK/dV kernel (4 KV rows per block, 256 threads) // ------------------------------------------------------------------ __global__ void dkdv_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ Q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ K, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ V, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dO, const float* __restrict__ M, const float* __restrict__ L, const float* __restrict__ D_vec, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dK, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dV, int B, int H, int H_kv, int N, float scale) { int b = blockIdx.z; int h_kv = blockIdx.y; int kv_base = blockIdx.x * KV_ROWS_PER_BLOCK; int tid = threadIdx.x; int row_offset = tid >> 6; // tid / 64 (0..3) int pair_idx = tid & 63; // tid % 64 (0..63) int d = pair_idx * 2; int abs_kv_row = kv_base + row_offset; int G = H / H_kv; int h_start = h_kv * G; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = K + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * HEAD_DIM; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = V + ((b * H_kv + h_kv) * N + abs_kv_row) * HEAD_DIM;
